  "content": "1: # Adding New Languages to Syntax Highlighting\n2: \n3: **Status:** Ready for Implementation  \n4: **Created:** 2025-12-15  \n5: **Prerequisites:** Familiarity with tree-sitter and Scheme-like query syntax\n6: \n7: ---\n8: \n9: ## Overview\n10: \n11: This document describes how to add support for new programming languages to the syntax highlighting system. The architecture is designed to make adding languages straightforward.\n12: \n13: ### Current Languages (17)\n14: \n15: | Language | Tree-sitter Crate | Query Source |\n16: |----------|-------------------|--------------|\n17: | YAML | `tree-sitter-yaml` | Custom: `queries/yaml/highlights.scm` |\n18: | Markdown | `tree-sitter-md` | Custom: `queries/markdown/highlights.scm` |\n19: | Rust | `tree-sitter-rust` | Built-in: `HIGHLIGHTS_QUERY` |\n20: | HTML | `tree-sitter-html` | Custom: `queries/html/highlights.scm` |\n21: | CSS | `tree-sitter-css` | Custom: `queries/css/highlights.scm` |\n22: | JavaScript | `tree-sitter-javascript` | Custom: `queries/javascript/highlights.scm` |\n23: | TypeScript | `tree-sitter-typescript` | Custom: `queries/typescript/highlights.scm` |\n24: | TSX | `tree-sitter-typescript` | Custom: `queries/typescript/highlights.scm` |\n25: | JSON | `tree-sitter-json` | Custom: `queries/json/highlights.scm` |\n26: | TOML | `tree-sitter-toml-ng` | Custom: `queries/toml/highlights.scm` |\n27: | Python | `tree-sitter-python` | Built-in: `HIGHLIGHTS_QUERY` |\n28: | Go | `tree-sitter-go` | Built-in: `HIGHLIGHTS_QUERY` |\n29: | PHP | `tree-sitter-php` | Built-in: `HIGHLIGHTS_QUERY` |\n30: | C | `tree-sitter-c` | Built-in: `HIGHLIGHT_QUERY` |\n31: | C++ | `tree-sitter-cpp` | Built-in: `HIGHLIGHT_QUERY` |\n32: | Java | `tree-sitter-java` | Built-in: `HIGHLIGHTS_QUERY` |\n33: | Bash | `tree-sitter-bash` | Built-in: `HIGHLIGHT_QUERY` |\n34: \n35: ### Future Languages\n36: \n37: **Potential additions:**\n38: - Swift (`tree-sitter-swift`)\n39: - Kotlin (`tree-sitter-kotlin`)\n40: - Ruby (`tree-sitter-ruby`)\n41: - SQL (`tree-sitter-sql`)\n42: - Lua (`tree-sitter-lua`)\n43: \n44: ---\n45: \n46: ## Step-by-Step: Adding a New Language\n47: \n48: ### Step 1: Add the tree-sitter grammar dependency\n49: \n50: Edit `Cargo.toml`:\n51: \n52: ```toml\n53: [dependencies]\n54: # ... existing deps ...\n55: tree-sitter-python = \"0.23\"  # Check crates.io for latest version\n56: ```\n57: \n58: ### Step 2: Add the LanguageId variant\n59: \n60: Edit `src/syntax/languages.rs`:\n61: \n62: ```rust\n63: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, Default)]\n64: pub enum LanguageId {\n65:     #[default]\n66:     PlainText,\n67:     // Phase 1 languages\n68:     Yaml,\n69:     Markdown,\n70:     Rust,\n71:     // Phase 2 languages (web stack)\n72:     Html,\n73:     Css,\n74:     JavaScript,\n75:     // Phase 3 languages -- ADD YOUR LANGUAGE HERE\n76:     Python,  // NEW\n77: }\n78: ```\n79: \n80: Update `from_extension()`:\n81: \n82: ```rust\n83: impl LanguageId {\n84:     pub fn from_extension(ext: &str) -> Self {\n85:         match ext.to_lowercase().as_str() {\n86:             // ... existing ...\n87:             \"py\" | \"pyw\" => LanguageId::Python,  // NEW\n88:             _ => LanguageId::PlainText,\n89:         }\n90:     }\n91: }\n92: ```\n93: \n94: Update `display_name()`:\n95: \n96: ```rust\n97: pub fn display_name(&self) -> &'static str {\n98:     match self {\n99:         // ... existing ...\n100:         LanguageId::Python => \"Python\",  // NEW\n101:     }\n102: }\n103: ```\n104: \n105: Update `has_highlighting()` if needed (defaults to true for non-PlainText).\n106: \n107: ### Step 3: Create the highlights query file\n108: \n109: Create `queries/python/highlights.scm`:\n110: \n111: ```scheme\n112: ; queries/python/highlights.scm\n113: ; Python syntax highlighting queries\n114: \n115: ; Keywords\n116: [\n117:   \"and\"\n118:   \"as\"\n119:   \"assert\"\n120:   \"async\"\n121:   \"await\"\n122:   \"break\"\n123:   \"class\"\n124:   \"continue\"\n125:   \"def\"\n126:   \"del\"\n127:   \"elif\"\n128:   \"else\"\n129:   \"except\"\n130:   \"finally\"\n131:   \"for\"\n132:   \"from\"\n133:   \"global\"\n134:   \"if\"\n135:   \"import\"\n136:   \"in\"\n137:   \"is\"\n138:   \"lambda\"\n139:   \"nonlocal\"\n140:   \"not\"\n141:   \"or\"\n142:   \"pass\"\n143:   \"raise\"\n144:   \"return\"\n145:   \"try\"\n146:   \"while\"\n147:   \"with\"\n148:   \"yield\"\n149: ] @keyword\n150: \n151: ; Function definitions\n152: (function_definition\n153:   name: (identifier) @function)\n154: \n155: ; Class definitions\n156: (class_definition\n157:   name: (identifier) @type)\n158: \n159: ; Function calls\n160: (call\n161:   function: (identifier) @function)\n162: \n163: ; Parameters\n164: (parameters\n165:   (identifier) @variable.parameter)\n166: \n167: ; Decorators\n168: (decorator) @attribute\n169: \n170: ; Strings\n171: (string) @string\n172: \n173: ; Comments\n174: (comment) @comment\n175: \n176: ; Numbers\n177: (integer) @number\n178: (float) @number\n179: \n180: ; Boolean\n181: (true) @boolean\n182: (false) @boolean\n183: (none) @constant.builtin\n184: \n185: ; Operators\n186: [\n187:   \"+\"\n188:   \"-\"\n189:   \"*\"\n190:   \"/\"\n191:   \"%\"\n192:   \"**\"\n193:   \"//\"\n194:   \"==\"\n195:   \"!=\"\n196:   \"<\"\n197:   \"<=\"\n198:   \">\"\n199:   \">=\"\n200:   \"=\"\n201:   \"+=\"\n202:   \"-=\"\n203:   \"*=\"\n204:   \"/=\"\n205: ] @operator\n206: \n207: ; Punctuation\n208: [\"(\" \")\" \"[\" \"]\" \"{\" \"}\"] @punctuation.bracket\n209: [\",\" \":\" \".\"] @punctuation.delimiter\n210: ```\n211: \n212: **Tips for writing queries:**\n213: - Use nvim-treesitter queries as reference: https://github.com/nvim-treesitter/nvim-treesitter/tree/master/queries\n214: - Run `tree-sitter parse <file>` to see the syntax tree structure\n215: - Test queries with tree-sitter playground: https://tree-sitter.github.io/tree-sitter/playground\n216: \n217: ### Step 4: Register the language in ParserState\n218: \n219: Edit `src/syntax/parser.rs`:\n220: \n221: Add the constant for the query file:\n222: \n223: ```rust\n224: // Embedded query files\n225: const YAML_HIGHLIGHTS: &str = include_str!(\"../../queries/yaml/highlights.scm\");\n226: // ... existing ...\n227: const PYTHON_HIGHLIGHTS: &str = include_str!(\"../../queries/python/highlights.scm\");  // NEW\n228: ```\n229: \n230: Update `ParserState::new()` to initialize the language:\n231: \n232: ```rust\n233: impl ParserState {\n234:     pub fn new() -> Self {\n235:         let mut state = Self {\n236:             parsers: HashMap::new(),\n237:             queries: HashMap::new(),\n238:             doc_cache: HashMap::new(),\n239:         };\n240: \n241:         // Initialize existing languages\n242:         state.init_language(LanguageId::Yaml);\n243:         state.init_language(LanguageId::Markdown);\n244:         state.init_language(LanguageId::Rust);\n245:         state.init_language(LanguageId::Html);\n246:         state.init_language(LanguageId::Css);\n247:         state.init_language(LanguageId::JavaScript);\n248:         \n249:         // Phase 3 languages -- ADD HERE\n250:         state.init_language(LanguageId::Python);  // NEW\n251: \n252:         state\n253:     }\n254: }\n255: ```\n256: \n257: Update `init_language()` match arm:\n258: \n259: ```rust\n260: fn init_language(&mut self, lang: LanguageId) {\n261:     let (ts_lang, highlights_scm) = match lang {\n262:         // ... existing ...\n263:         LanguageId::Python => (\n264:             tree_sitter_python::LANGUAGE.into(),\n265:             PYTHON_HIGHLIGHTS,\n266:         ),\n267:         LanguageId::PlainText => return,\n268:     };\n269:     // ... rest of function ...\n270: }\n271: ```\n272: \n273: ### Step 5: Add the query compilation test\n274: \n275: Edit `src/syntax/parser.rs`, add to `query_compilation_tests` module:\n276: \n277: ```rust\n278: #[test]\n279: fn test_python_query_compiles() {\n280:     assert_query_compiles(\n281:         \"Python\",\n282:         tree_sitter_python::LANGUAGE.into(),\n283:         PYTHON_HIGHLIGHTS,\n284:     );\n285: }\n286: ```\n287: \n288: Also update `test_all_query_files_compile()`:\n289: \n290: ```rust\n291: let languages_with_queries = [\n292:     LanguageId::Yaml,\n293:     LanguageId::Markdown,\n294:     LanguageId::Rust,\n295:     LanguageId::Html,\n296:     LanguageId::Css,\n297:     LanguageId::JavaScript,\n298:     LanguageId::Python,  // NEW\n299: ];\n300: ```\n301: \n302: ### Step 6: Add a parsing test\n303: \n304: Add a test in `src/syntax/parser.rs`:\n305: \n306: ```rust\n307: #[test]\n308: fn test_python_parsing() {\n309:     let mut state = ParserState::new();\n310:     let source = r#\"\n311: def greet(name):\n312:     \"\"\"Say hello.\"\"\"\n313:     print(f\"Hello, {name}!\")\n314: \n315: class Person:\n316:     def __init__(self, name):\n317:         self.name = name\n318: \n319: if __name__ == \"__main__\":\n320:     greet(\"World\")\n321: \"#;\n322:     let doc_id = DocumentId(100);\n323:     let highlights = state.parse_and_highlight(source, LanguageId::Python, doc_id, 1);\n324: \n325:     assert_eq!(highlights.language, LanguageId::Python);\n326:     assert!(!highlights.lines.is_empty());\n327: }\n328: ```\n329: \n330: ### Step 7: Run tests and verify\n331: \n332: ```bash\n333: # Run all syntax tests\n334: cargo test --lib syntax\n335: \n336: # Run just the new language tests\n337: cargo test --lib python\n338: \n339: # Run benchmarks to verify performance\n340: cargo bench --bench syntax -- parse_sample\n341: ```\n342: \n343: ---\n344: \n345: ## Supported Highlight Names\n346: \n347: The editor maps tree-sitter capture names to theme colors. These are the supported captures in `HIGHLIGHT_NAMES`:\n348: \n349: ```\n350: attribute             - @attribute (decorators, annotations)\n351: boolean               - @boolean (true, false)\n352: comment               - @comment\n353: constant              - @constant\n354: constant.builtin      - @constant.builtin (null, nil, None)\n355: constructor           - @constructor (new Foo)\n356: escape                - @escape (string escapes like \\n)\n357: function              - @function\n358: function.builtin      - @function.builtin (print, len)\n359: function.method       - @function.method\n360: keyword               - @keyword\n361: keyword.return        - @keyword.return\n362: keyword.function      - @keyword.function (def, fn, function)\n363: keyword.operator      - @keyword.operator (and, or, not)\n364: label                 - @label (goto labels, YAML anchors)\n365: number                - @number\n366: operator              - @operator\n367: property              - @property\n368: punctuation           - @punctuation (general)\n369: punctuation.bracket   - @punctuation.bracket\n370: punctuation.delimiter - @punctuation.delimiter\n371: punctuation.special   - @punctuation.special\n372: string                - @string\n373: string.special        - @string.special (regex, heredoc)\n374: tag                   - @tag (HTML tags)\n375: tag.attribute         - @tag.attribute\n376: text                  - @text (plain text in markdown)\n377: text.emphasis         - @text.emphasis (*italic*)\n378: text.strong           - @text.strong (**bold**)\n379: text.title            - @text.title (headings)\n380: text.uri              - @text.uri (URLs)\n381: type                  - @type\n382: type.builtin          - @type.builtin (int, str, bool)\n383: variable              - @variable\n384: variable.builtin      - @variable.builtin (self, this)\n385: variable.parameter    - @variable.parameter\n386: ```\n387: \n388: If your query uses a capture name not in this list, it will be silently ignored. You can either:\n389: 1. Map it to an existing capture (e.g., `@keyword.control` → `@keyword`)\n390: 2. Add the new capture to `HIGHLIGHT_NAMES` in `src/syntax/highlights.rs`\n391: \n392: ---\n393: \n394: ## Query File Locations\n395: \n396: Queries are embedded at compile time via `include_str!()`:\n397: \n398: ```\n399: queries/\n400: ├── yaml/\n401: │   └── highlights.scm\n402: ├── markdown/\n403: │   └── highlights.scm\n404: ├── html/\n405: │   └── highlights.scm\n406: ├── css/\n407: │   └── highlights.scm\n408: ├── javascript/\n409: │   └── highlights.scm\n410: └── python/           # NEW\n411:     └── highlights.scm\n412: ```\n413: \n414: **Note:** Rust uses the built-in `tree_sitter_rust::HIGHLIGHTS_QUERY` constant instead of a separate file.\n415: \n416: ---\n417: \n418: ## Debugging Query Issues\n419: \n420: ### Query doesn't compile\n421: \n422: Run the specific test:\n423: \n424: ```bash\n425: cargo test --lib test_python_query_compiles\n426: ```\n427: \n428: The test will show:\n429: - Exact row and column of the error\n430: - The problematic line\n431: - Error type (invalid capture, syntax error, etc.)\n432: \n433: ### Highlights aren't appearing\n434: \n435: 1. **Check the language is detected:**\n436:    ```rust\n437:    let lang = LanguageId::from_extension(\"py\");\n438:    println!(\"{:?}\", lang);  // Should be Python, not PlainText\n439:    ```\n440: \n441: 2. **Check the query has captures:**\n442:    Run the debug overlay (F8) and look at \"Syntax Highlighting\" section.\n443: \n444: 3. **Check capture names match:**\n445:    Use `HIGHLIGHT_NAMES` captures only, or add new ones.\n446: \n447: 4. **Inspect the syntax tree:**\n448:    Use `tree-sitter parse file.py` to see the actual tree structure.\n449: \n450: ### Performance issues\n451: \n452: Run benchmarks:\n453: \n454: ```bash\n455: cargo bench --bench syntax -- python\n456: ```\n457: \n458: If parsing is slow:\n459: - Reduce query complexity\n460: - Use more specific patterns\n461: - Consider lazy loading for rarely-used languages\n462: \n463: ---\n464: \n465: ## Resources\n466: \n467: - [Tree-sitter Documentation](https://tree-sitter.github.io/tree-sitter/)\n468: - [Tree-sitter Query Syntax](https://tree-sitter.github.io/tree-sitter/using-parsers#pattern-matching-with-queries)\n469: - [nvim-treesitter queries](https://github.com/nvim-treesitter/nvim-treesitter/tree/master/queries) - High-quality reference queries\n470: - [Tree-sitter Playground](https://tree-sitter.github.io/tree-sitter/playground) - Test queries interactively",

Window dimensions are correctly handled in physical pixels\n - Mouse input is correctly received in physical pixels\n - Character widths and line heights are correctly computed from scaled fonts\n \n-### What's Broken\n+### What Was Fixed (v0.3.4)\n \n-- **UI layout constants don't scale**: `TAB_BAR_HEIGHT`, `SPLITTER_WIDTH`, padding values are hardcoded in pixels\n-- **Scale factor isn't stored**: Can't be used after initialization\n-- **No scale factor change handling**: Moving window between monitors breaks UI\n-- **Inconsistent coordinate types**: No semantic distinction between physical/logical pixels\n+- **UI layout constants now scale**: `ScaledMetrics` struct holds all scaled values\n+- **Scale factor is stored**: `model.metrics.scale_factor` available throughout\n+- **Scale factor change handling**: `ScaleFactorChanged` event triggers full renderer reinitialization\n+- **Surface properly resized**: New Surface explicitly resized after creation\n+- **Dynamic tab bar height**: Computed from glyph metrics (`line_height + padding * 2`)\n+- **Buffer bounds checking**: `Frame::new` validates buffer size to prevent panics\n \n-### Impact\n+### Previous Impact (Now Fixed)\n \n-On a 2x Retina display:\n-- Tab bar appears half the intended size (28px physical = 14pt logical)\n-- Gutter padding appears too tight\n-- Click targets for tabs may be misaligned\n-- Overall UI looks cramped while text looks correct\n+On a 2x Retina display (before fix):\n+- Tab bar appeared half the intended size (28px physical = 14pt logical)\n+- Gutter padding appeared too tight\n+- Click targets for tabs were misaligned\n+- Overall UI looked cramped while text looked correct\n+- Switching displays caused incorrect rendering until window resize\n \n ---\n \n ## Current Architecture Analysis\n```",
